# Counterexamples to the Hahn-Wilson conjecture at height two

Coordinate `EN-104-000-0007-2-2026-PR-T4-UK-EX-M3-C-AL-H-0644` | `PM-319.1` | status ACTIVE | original slug `Counterexamples-to-the-Hahn-Wilson-conjecture-at-height-two-September-26-2026` | date 2026-09-26 | derived from the openai/math preprint Counterexamples-to-the-Hahn-Wilson-conjecture-at-height-two-September-26-2026, modified (Apache-2.0). ```json
{
  "abstract_verbatim": "We disprove the Hahn–Wilson conjecture at height two. For every sufficiently large prime <i>p</i>, we construct a connective <i>p</i>-complete spectrum <i>X</i> of exact fp-type two outside the ordinary thick subcategory generated by the specified standard form of $`\\mathrm{BP}\\langle2\\rangle_p^\\wedge`$. The same spectrum satisfies both localization comparisons $`L_2^fX\\simeq L_2X`$ and $`L_{T(2)}X\\simeq L_{K(2)}X`$.",

<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We disprove the Hahn–Wilson conjecture at height two. For every sufficiently large prime <i>p</i>, we construct a connective <i>p</i>-complete spectrum <i>X</i> of exact fp-type two outside the ordinary thick subcategory generated by the specified standard form of $`\mathrm{BP}\langle2\rangle_p^\wedge`$. The same spectrum satisfies both localization comparisons $`L_2^fX\simeq L_2X`$ and $`L_{T(2)}X\simeq L_{K(2)}X`$.

- **claim-main-theorem** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For every sufficiently large prime $p$, there is a connective $p$-complete spectrum $X$ of exact fp-type two such that, for a standard form of $\mathrm{BP}\langle2\rangle_p^\wedge$, $$X\notin\mathop{\mathrm{Thick}}(\mathrm{BP}\langle2\rangle_p^\wedge).$$ The same spectrum satisfies $$L_2^fX\simeq L_2X, \qquad L_{T(2)}X\simeq L_{K(2)}X.$$
  - Explanation: For all large primes a connective p-complete spectrum of exact fp-type two lies outside the ordinary thick closure of the standard height-two truncated Brown–Peterson spectrum, with both localization comparisons.
- **claim-law-charts-flat** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geom-law-charts)): The map $f$ is flat and affine. The maps $\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}A_2\to\mathcal M$ and $\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}A_{\mathrm{Haz}}\to\mathcal M$ are flat affine covers. In graded coordinates, the cooperation Hopf algebroid of the latter chart has arrow ring $$A_{\mathrm{Haz}}\otimes_{P,\eta_L}\Gamma_P \otimes_{P,\eta_R}A_{\mathrm{Haz}}.$$
  - Explanation: The chart map is flat and affine and the Araki and Hazewinkel charts are flat affine covers.
- **claim-torsor-quotient** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geom-torsor)): Let $A$ be a $k$-algebra with a locally finite continuous action of $Z$, and let $Z_m$ be the subgroup of index $p^m$. Suppose that $$A=\bigcup_{m\geq0}A^{Z_m}$$ and that the action on $\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}A$ is geometrically free. Then $\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}A\to\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(A^Z)$ is an fpqc $Z$-torsor.
  - Explanation: A locally finite geometrically free action of the procyclic group gives a torsor over the invariants.
- **claim-rigidification** (construction, [source](#stmt-geom-rigidification)): There is a flat affine groupoid stack $\mathcal N$ and a map $r:\mathcal M_k\to\mathcal N$ that quotients the vertical central $Z$-action on arrows. Its pullback is exact and identifies sheaves on $\mathcal N$ with sheaves on $\mathcal M_k$ having trivial vertical $Z$-action. Moreover $$f'=rf_k:\mathcal X_k\longrightarrow\mathcal N$$ is flat and affine. The weight line descends to $\mathcal N$, and $t=v_1$ is an invariant Cartier section of $\mathcal O(1)$ there.
  - Explanation: Quotienting the vertical central action gives a flat affine groupoid stack with chart, weight line and Cartier section.
- **claim-height-one-open** (classification, [source](#stmt-geom-open)): There are equivalences $$\mathcal M_0[1/t]\simeq BZ,\qquad \mathcal N[1/t]\simeq\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}k.$$ The first is split by the law with $v_1=1$ and $v_{i>1}=0$.
  - Explanation: Inverting t gives the classifying stack of the central group, and the spectrum of k after rigidification.
- **claim-thickened-charts** (classification, [source](#stmt-geom-thickened-charts)): The chart law $F_x$ with $v_1=x$, $v_2=1$, and $v_{i>2}=0$ gives compatible presentations $$\label{geom:quotient-thickenings} \mathcal M_k/(t^d)=[\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}R_d/G_{\mathrm{up}}],\qquad \mathcal N/(t^d)=[\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}R_d/G].$$ Under these presentations, $\mathcal X_k/(t^d)$ is $[\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}R_d/\Lambda]$. Sheaves are smooth, that is, discrete continuous, semilinear representations over $R_d$. The compatible coefficient action on $R$ satisfies $$g(x)\in x+x^2R\quad(g\in H),\qquad \lambda(x)=\lambda^{-1}x\quad(\lambda\in\Lambda).$$ If $u$ is the chart basis of the weight line with $t=xu$, then $$\label{geom:twist-cocycle} g(u)=\frac{x}{g(x)}u\quad(g\in H),\qquad \lambda(u)=\lambda u\quad(\lambda\in\Lambda).$$ For every strict label representing an element $g\in H$, there is a formal isomorphism $F_{g(x)}\to F_x$ over $R$ reducing to that label.
  - Explanation: Nilpotent thickenings are quotient stacks of truncated power series rings by the stabilizer with an explicit semilinear action.
- **claim-coinduction-reductions** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geom-coinduction)): The sheaves $T'$ and $T_k$ are $t$-monic. Set $T'_d=T'/t^d$ and $T_{k,d}=T_k/t^d$; their ordinary quotients also represent their derived multiplication cofibers. On the chart of Proposition <a href="#geom:thickened-charts" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="geom:thickened-charts">6</a>, $$T'_d=C_{\mathrm{lc}}(G/\Lambda,R_d) =C_{\mathrm{lc}}(H,R_d),$$ with action $$(gF)(s)=g\bigl(F(g^{-1}s)\bigr) \qquad(g\in G,\ s\in G/\Lambda).$$ The inner value is acted on through the coefficient action on $R_d$. The analogous description of $T_{k,d}$ uses $G_{\mathrm{up}}/\Lambda$.
  - Explanation: Chart sheaves are t-monic and their reductions are coinduced modules of locally constant functions.
- **lemma-group-filtration** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geom-group-filtration)): The filtration is complete and separated, and $$\label{geom:group-graded} H(i)/H(i+1)= \begin{cases} (k,+),&i\text{ odd},\\ (k/\mathbb F_p,+),&i\text{ even}, \end{cases} \qquad [H(i),H(j)]\subset H(i+j).$$ The $p$-th-power map carries each graded digit to the identical digit two indices later. Choose $g_1,g_2$ with first digits an $\mathbb F_p$-basis of $k$, and choose $g_3=\gamma$ with only nonzero digit $t_2=\theta\notin\mathbb F_p$. Then $$(a_1,a_2,a_3)\longmapsto g_1^{a_1}g_2^{a_2}g_3^{a_3}$$ gives unique coordinates $\mathbb Z_p^3\to H$. Modulo $\Gamma_s$, the coordinates are taken modulo $p^s$. In particular $\Gamma_s$ consists of coordinates divisible by $p^s$, and $H(2)$ consists of coordinates whose first two entries are divisible by $p$. The element $\gamma$ commutes with $\Lambda$.
  - Explanation: The digit filtration is complete and separated with explicit graded pieces and unique p-adic coordinates.
- **claim-skew-filtration** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geom-skew-filtration)): The ring $\Delta$ has a complete separated decreasing filtration given by the ordered weights $$\operatorname{wt}(x)=4,\qquad \operatorname{wt}(U_1)=\operatorname{wt}(U_2)=1,\qquad \operatorname{wt}(U_3)=3.$$ Its associated graded is the commutative polynomial ring $$\label{geom:graded-operations} \mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\Delta=k[\sigma x,A,B,C].$$ The element $x$ is normal, $\Omega=\Delta/(x)$, and the induced filtration gives $\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\Omega=k[A,B,C]$. Here $A,B,C$ are the initial symbols of $U_1,U_2,U_3$.
  - Explanation: The completed skew group ring has a complete separated weight filtration with commutative polynomial associated graded ring.
- **lemma-injective-model** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geom-injective-model)): For each flat affine groupoid stack used here, twisted periodic complexes admit the injective model structure with quasi-isomorphisms as weak equivalences and monomorphisms as cofibrations. It is stable, its fibrant objects have injective sheaf terms, and maps into a fibrant object are computed up to compatible chain homotopy. If a sheaf $W$ satisfies $\mathop{\mathrm{Ext}}^{>a}(W,-)=0$ for some finite $a$, maps from its pure shifts can be computed by applying $\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(W,-)$ termwise to any quasi-isomorphic replacement whose terms are $\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(W,-)$-acyclic.
  - Explanation: Twisted periodic complexes carry a stable injective model structure computing maps by chain homotopy.
- **lemma-sections-resolution** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geom-sections-resolution)): The right derived functors of sections in the sheaf categories above commute with filtered colimits and are computed by the flat affine coinduced resolution. The resolution is compatible with closed base change. If $\mathop{\mathrm{Ext}}^{>a}(\mathcal O,-)=0$, its finite truncation gives a sections-acyclic resolution that computes <a href="#geom:sections-homotopy" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="geom:sections-homotopy">[geom:sections-homotopy]</a> on periodic complexes. In particular $\pi_*$ then commutes with filtered colimits and telescopes.
  - Explanation: Derived sections commute with filtered colimits and are computed by a finite coinduced resolution under an Ext bound.
- **lemma-group-cohomology-dimension** (bound, [source](#stmt-geom-group-cohomology)): Invariantsof smooth $k$-linear $H$-modules have cohomological dimension at most $3$. The same is true for $G$ and for sections on $\mathcal N/(t^d)$, for every $d\geq1$.
  - Explanation: Invariants of smooth modules over the stabilizer and nilpotent charts have bounded cohomological dimension.
- **claim-cohomological-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-geom-cohomological-bounds)): Projective dimensions in the Ext sense satisfy $$\label{geom:dimension-bounds} \operatorname{pd}_{\mathcal N}(\mathcal O)\leq4,\qquad \operatorname{pd}_{\mathcal M_k}(\mathcal O), \operatorname{pd}_{\mathcal M_0}(\mathcal O)\leq5,\qquad \operatorname{pd}_{\mathcal M}(\mathcal O)\leq6.$$ For sheaves on $\mathcal M_k$, the right adjoint of $r^*$ has $$\label{geom:central-derived-invariants} r_*M=\ker(c-1:M\to M),\qquad R^1r_*M=\mathop{\mathrm{coker}}(c-1:M\to M),\qquad R^{>1}r_*M=0,$$ where the kernel and cokernel descend to $\mathcal N$.
  - Explanation: The structure sheaf has bounded Ext projective dimension on each stack; central invariants form a two-term delta functor.
- **claim-central-triangle** (construction, [source](#stmt-geom-relative-pushforward)): Choose an affine law chart $l:S\to\mathcal M_k$, set $I=l_*l^*$, and let $J$ be the cokernel of its unit. The two-term totalization $$\label{geom:Q-definition} Q=\bigl[r_*I\longrightarrow r_*J\bigr], \qquad\text{in homological degrees }0,-1,$$ computes $Rr_*$ on twisted periodic complexes and preserves quasi-isomorphisms. There are natural identifications $$QT_k\simeq T',\qquad \pi_*(r^*U)\cong\pi_*(Qr^*U)\quad(U\in\mathbf D(\mathcal N)),$$ and natural triangles $$\label{geom:central-triangle} U\longrightarrow Qr^*U\longrightarrow U[-1] \longrightarrow U[1].$$
  - Explanation: A two-term totalization computes the central pushforward and gives a natural, possibly nonsplit comparison triangle.
- **claim-fracture-square** (theorem, [source](#stmt-complete-fracture)): The functor $L$ is the flat affine restriction and pushforward for the open $t\ne 0$, whose derived category it embeds fully faithfully. Completion is exact and idempotent. Its values are right orthogonal, in every shift, to $t$-invertible objects. For every $P$, the fiber of $P\to\widehat P$ is $t$-invertible, and there are natural equivalences $$P/t^d\simeq\widehat P/t^d \qquad(d\geq 1).$$ The following square is a homotopy pullback: $$\label{complete:fracture-square} \begin{tikzcd}[column sep=large,row sep=large] P \arrow[r] \arrow[d] & \widehat P \arrow[d]\\ LP \arrow[r] & L\widehat P. \end{tikzcd}$$ Thus the original object is recovered by matching its open and complete parts over the localized completion. Moreover, $$\label{complete:localization-sections} \pi_i LP=\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{s\geq 0}\pi_{i+sD}P.$$
  - Explanation: Completion is exact and idempotent and every object is the homotopy pullback of its localization and completion.
- **lemma-open-splitting** (classification, [source](#stmt-complete-open-splitting)): The category $\mathbf D(\mathcal N[1/t])$ is the category of $D$-periodic complexes of $k$-vector spaces. Every object is the sum, over the $D$ residue classes, of its pure homology objects, and every graded periodic map on homology is realizable.* *The category $\mathbf D(\mathcal M_0[1/t])$ is the category of $D$-periodic complexes of smooth $Z$-representations over $\mathbb F_p$. Every object also splits as the sum of its pure homology objects. Maps can have an $\mathop{\mathrm{Ext}}^1$ component, but a pure object has no self-map to its $2$-shift when $D>3$.
  - Explanation: Objects over the open split into pure homology pieces with no self-map to the 2-shift.
- **claim-complete-operations** (classification, [source](#stmt-complete-operations)): There is an identification of graded algebras $$\label{complete:operation-algebra} \mathscr E_{sD}=(x^{-s}\Delta)^\Lambda\quad(s\in\mathbb Z), \qquad \mathscr E_n=0\quad(n\notin D\mathbb Z).$$ Products use left-coefficient skew convolution in $\Delta[x^{-1}]$, with degrees added. Every element of $\mathscr E_{sD}$ has a unique compatible system of ordinary sheaf-map representatives $T'_d\to T'_d(s)$, and these representatives realize multiplication strictly. Under <a href="#complete:operation-algebra" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="complete:operation-algebra">[complete:operation-algebra]</a>, the operation $t$ of degree $D$ is the coefficient $1\in x^{-1}\Delta$; it is central in $\mathscr E$.* *Put $$A_{\mathrm{op}}=(\Delta[x^{-1}])^\Lambda.$$ With a formal $t$ recording degree $D$, localization identifies $$\label{complete:localized-operations} \mathscr E[t^{-1}]=A_{\mathrm{op}}[t^{\pm1}].$$
  - Explanation: The endomorphism algebra of the completed generator is given by invariant fractional lattices in the localized skew ring with strict representatives.
- **cor-degree-two-vanishing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-complete-degree-two)): For each $j\in\mathbb Z$ there are no self-maps to the $2$-shift of $\widehat T'[j]$, $\widehat T_k[j]$, or $\widehat T_0[j]$.
  - Explanation: The three completed chart sheaves have no self-maps to their 2-shifts in any shift.
- **claim-strict-realization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-complete-realization)): There is an exact fully faithful functor $$\mathcal R:\mathop{\mathrm{Perf}}(\mathscr E)\longrightarrow\mathbf D(\mathcal N)$$ from perfect right dg modules which sends $\mathscr E$ to $\widehat T'$ and identifies its image with $\mathop{\mathrm{Thick}}(\widehat T')$. For a finite-cell module $\mathsf P$, its image is the homotopy limit of an actual compatible tower $Y_d$ of complexes whose terms are finite sums of shifts of $T'_d$.
  - Explanation: Perfect dg modules over the operation algebra embed fully faithfully with image the thick closure of the completed generator.
- **claim-section-formulas** (theorem, [source](#stmt-complete-sections)): If $Y=\mathcal R(\mathsf P)$ for a perfect right dg $\mathscr E$-module $\mathsf P$, then naturally $$\label{complete:tensor-formulas} \begin{split} \pi_*Y&=H_*\bigl(\mathsf P\otimes^{\mathbf L}_{\mathscr E} \mathsf L\bigr),\\ W(Y)&=H_*\bigl(\mathsf P\otimes^{\mathbf L}_{\mathscr E} \Phi[t^{\pm1}]\bigr). \end{split}$$ For a finite-cell model the ordinary cellular tensor computes each derived tensor. The actions in these formulas are the left actions of <a href="#complete:action-on-sections" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="complete:action-on-sections">[complete:action-on-sections]</a>.
  - Explanation: Sections of a realized object are derived tensor products with explicit coefficient lattices.
- **lemma-tower-is-reduction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-complete-reductions)): For a finite-cell realization $Y=\mathop{\mathrm{holim}}_eY_e$ there are natural equivalences $$Y/t^d\simeq Y_d\qquad(d\geq1).$$ Under these equivalences, the tower projection is the intrinsic reduction map. The profinite topology on $\pi_iY$ obtained from the finite-level sections calculation is consequently its inverse-limit topology from $\pi_i(Y/t^d)$.* *If $Y'$ is a retract of $Y$, let $e_d$ be the idempotent of $Y_d$ in the derived category induced by the idempotent defining this retract. Then $Y'/t^d$ is the summand of $Y_d$ split by $e_d$, and $$\pi_iY'=\varprojlim_d\operatorname{im}(\pi_i e_d)$$ with its intrinsic profinite topology.
  - Explanation: The realization tower agrees with the intrinsic reductions and gives the profinite topology, also for retracts.
- **claim-overlap-topology** (theorem, [source](#stmt-complete-topology)): Every $P\in\mathop{\mathrm{Thick}}(T')$ has finite $\pi_iP$ for every integer $i$. Now let $P\in\mathbf D(\mathcal N)$ have finite $\pi_iP$ for every integer $i$. For each integer $i$, the group $\pi_i\widehat P$ is naturally profinite, with the intrinsic inverse-limit topology from the finite groups $\pi_i(\widehat P/t^d)$. The vector space $W_i(\widehat P)=\pi_iL\widehat P$ has a locally compact Hausdorff $k$-vector-space topology obtained as the increasing union of the compact open images of $\pi_{i+sD}\widehat P$, for $s\geq0$. The image of $\pi_iLP$ is discrete and cocompact in this space.* *If $Q\in\mathbf D(\mathcal N)$ also has finite $\pi_jQ$ for every integer $j$, then every map $f:\widehat P\to\widehat Q$ induces continuous maps $\pi_i\widehat P\to\pi_i\widehat Q$ and $W_i(\widehat P)\to W_i(\widehat Q)$ for every integer $i$, in the topologies just described. No global map $P\to Q$ inducing $f$ is required. If $\widehat P\simeq\mathcal R(\mathsf P)$ for a perfect right dg $\mathscr E$-module $\mathsf P$, then under this identification the natural identifications of Proposition <a href="#complete:sections" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="complete:sections">20</a> and Lemma <a href="#complete:reductions" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="complete:reductions">21</a> match the profinite topology on $\pi_i\widehat P$ with the one obtained from the realization’s finite-level sections and intrinsic reductions. Consequently they match the compact images and the increasing-union topology on $W_i(\widehat P)$.
  - Explanation: Degreewise finiteness gives profinite completed homotopy and a locally compact overlap with discrete cocompact open image; maps are continuous.
- **lemma-displacement-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-op-displacement)): If $g\in H(i)\setminus H(i+1)$, then $$\ell(g)\le r_i:=\sum_{j=1}^i p^{\lfloor j/2\rfloor}. \label{op:digit-displacement}$$ The action of $H$ on $R$ is faithful. With $r_0=0$, one has $$\kappa:=\sum_{l\ge1}\frac1{N_l}\le\frac p{p-1}. \label{op:kappa}$$ Moreover, for every $s\ge1$, $$\Gamma_s\subset H_{p^s}\subset H(2s-1), \qquad N_{p^s}\ge p^{3(s-1)}. \label{op:congruence-index}$$
  - Explanation: The first nonidentity digit controls the congruence level, giving faithfulness and summable reciprocal indices.
- **lemma-divided-differences** (construction, [source](#stmt-op-divided-differences)): For $m\ge0$, the finite measure $$D_m=\sum_{i=0}^m \frac{x^{r'_m}}{\prod_{0\le j\le m,\ j\ne i}(u_i-u_j)} \,\delta_{b_i^H} \label{op:measure}$$ belongs to $\Delta$. Its moments satisfy $$\int_H D_m(g)u_g^n= \begin{cases} 0,&0\le n<m,\\ x^{r'_m},&n=m, \end{cases} \qquad \int_H D_m(g)u_g^n\in x^{r'_m+n-m}R\quad(n>m).$$
  - Explanation: Divided-difference measures on balanced orbit points are integral and prescribe a single moment.
- **lemma-coefficient-control** (construction, [source](#stmt-op-coefficient-control)): Suppose <a href="#op:parameters" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="op:parameters">[op:parameters]</a> holds, $X=p^r\le qb<pX$, and $b\ge X/(2M)$. By adding a measure in $(x^{X+1}\Delta)^\Lambda$ to $\nu$, one may change $f_b(z)$ by any polynomial of degree at most $M$ in $z_r$, independently for every lower label $z\bmod X$, without changing any coefficient function of smaller depth. Successive such corrections over increasing depths converge in $\Delta$ and do not change reduction modulo $x$.
  - Explanation: Corrections modify one depth of the coefficient function by low-degree polynomials without disturbing smaller depths.
- **lemma-initialization** (construction, [source](#stmt-op-initialization)): There is a measure $\nu\in\Delta^\Lambda$ with reduction $\delta_\gamma-\delta_1$ such that $$\int_H\nu(g)u_g^n=0\quad(0\le n<p^2).$$ For this initial measure, $\int_H\nu(g)h_g^z\in x^{p^2}R$ for every $z\in\mathbb Z_p$. After any convergent sequence of the corrections in Lemma <a href="#op:coefficient-control" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="op:coefficient-control">25</a>, the weighted operator $a$ satisfies $$a\bmod x=\delta_\gamma-\delta_1=U_3, \qquad \sigma(a)=C. \label{op:initial-symbol}$$
  - Explanation: An initial invariant measure with prescribed reduction and vanishing low moments fixes the initial symbol.
- **lemma-rank-matching** (classification, [source](#stmt-op-matching)): For such a matrix, the numbers $$\mathsf R(l,j)-\mathsf R(l,j-1)-\mathsf R(l+1,j) +\mathsf R(l+1,j-1) \label{op:rank-matching}$$ are $0$ or $1$ and define a partial matching of rows with columns. The rank $\mathsf R(l,j)$ counts the matched pairs in its block. When entries are fixed in order of increasing depth $b=j-l$, a new match at $(l,j)$ is possible exactly when both its row and column are still unmatched and the corner entry avoids one specified scalar. That scalar is determined by smaller-depth entries. Decisions at a fixed depth are independent of one another.
  - Explanation: Double differences of block ranks of a triangular matrix define a partial matching of rows and columns.
- **claim-operator-persistent-defects** (construction, [source](#stmt-op-operator)): Under <a href="#op:parameters" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="op:parameters">[op:parameters]</a>, there is $a\in\mathscr E_0$ satisfying <a href="#op:initial-symbol" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="op:initial-symbol">[op:initial-symbol]</a> whose triangular matrix on $\Phi$ has the following properties.* 1. *For every fixed $J\in\mathbb Z$, only finitely many matched pairs $(l,j)$ satisfy $l\le J<j$.* 2. *There are constants $c>0$ and $N_0$ such that for every integer $N\ge N_0$, at least $cN^\beta$ columns in $[-N,0]$ and at least $cN^\beta$ rows in $(0,N]$ are forever unmatched.* *Here the matching is that of Lemma <a href="#op:matching" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="op:matching">27</a>; the constants may depend on $p$.
  - Explanation: An operator exists with finitely many matched crossings of any cut and many unmatched rows and columns at every large scale.
- **lemma-pivot-topology** (theorem, [source](#stmt-glue-pivot-topology)): An integer $j$ is an unmatched column of $a$ if and only if there is a vector in $\mathcal K$ with leading term $w^j$. An integer $l$ is a matched row if and only if it is the leading position of a nonzero vector in $a\Phi$. Moreover, for every $J\in\mathbb Z$, $$\dim_k\frac{a\Phi\cap F_J\Phi}{a(F_J\Phi)} =\#\{(l,j)\text{ matched}:l\le J<j\}<\infty. \label{glue:crossing-quotient}$$ The image $a\Phi$ is closed. Thus $\mathcal Q$ and $\mathcal K$ are Hausdorff locally compact $k$-vector spaces, with compact open subspaces given respectively by the images of $F_J\Phi$ and by $\mathcal K\cap F_J\Phi$.
  - Explanation: Unmatched columns and matched rows correspond to kernel and image leading terms; the image is closed.
- **lemma-overlap-cone** (theorem, [source](#stmt-glue-overlap)): For every degree $i$, the normalized overlap has an identification $$W_i(Y)=\mathcal Q\oplus\mathcal K, \label{glue:overlap-splitting}$$ compatible with the $t$-period identifications. The map $\pi_iY\longrightarrow W_i(Y)$ has finite kernel and compact open image. Each such image is commensurable with $$C_0^{\rm lat}=\mathop{\mathrm{im}}_{\mathcal Q}(F_0\Phi) \oplus(\mathcal K\cap F_0\Phi). \label{glue:standard-compact}$$ The topology of $\pi_iY$ is the intrinsic compact topology from its finite reductions, and the induced topology on the overlap is the locally compact topology in <a href="#glue:overlap-splitting" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="glue:overlap-splitting">[glue:overlap-splitting]</a>. Every fixed compact stage of this overlap is contained in the image of one sufficiently late $t$-translated group $\pi_{i+sD}Y$.
  - Explanation: The overlap of the cone splits into cokernel and kernel with compact open commensurable stages.
- **lemma-defect-pairs** (theorem, [source](#stmt-glue-pairs)): The vectors $(q_j,k_l)\in\mathcal Q\oplus\mathcal K$, indexed by the paired lists, are linearly independent modulo $C_0^{\rm lat}$. For every sufficiently large integer $N$, at least $cN^\beta$ pairs, for a fixed $c>0$, simultaneously satisfy $j\le N$ and $\|l\|\le N$. For these pairs, their kernel coordinates are linearly independent modulo $x^{qN+1}R$.
  - Explanation: Paired defect vectors are independent modulo the standard compact lattice and many lie in a bounded window.
- **claim-glued-object** (construction, [source](#stmt-glue-object)): The object $U$ of <a href="#glue:definition-U" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="glue:definition-U">[glue:definition-U]</a> satisfies $$\pi_iU\text{ is finite for every }i,\qquad \widehat U\simeq Y\in\mathop{\mathrm{Thick}}(\widehat T'),\qquad LU\simeq V.$$ Under these equivalences the structure map $LU\longrightarrow L\widehat U$ is the chosen lattice map.
  - Explanation: The glued object has degreewise finite homotopy, completes to a finite construction and localizes to the open piece.
- **lemma-global-map-constraint** (theorem, [source](#stmt-glue-global-maps)): For every map $g:U\to P$ with $P\in\mathop{\mathrm{Thick}}(T')$, the induced map $$W_i(\widehat g):\mathcal Q\oplus\mathcal K \longrightarrow W_i(\widehat P)$$ is continuous and sends each paired vector $(q_j,k_l)$ into the discrete lattice $\mathop{\mathrm{im}}(\pi_iLP\to W_i(\widehat P))$.
  - Explanation: A global map to the thick closure sends paired defect vectors into the discrete lattice.
- **thm-finite-tower-criterion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rew-obs-tower-criterion)): Let $\mathcal C$ be an idempotent-complete triangulated category, let $T_0,U_0\in\mathcal C$, and let $$\Psi:\mathcal C\longrightarrow\mathop{\mathrm{Perf}}(\mathscr B_0)$$ be an exact functor with $\Psi(T_0)\simeq\mathscr B_0$. Assume that $H(\Psi(U_0))$ is torsion as a right $S_0$-module in every degree and is nonzero in at least one degree. Assume also that, for every $P\in\mathop{\mathrm{Thick}}_{\mathcal C}(T_0)$ and every map $g:U_0\to P$ in $\mathcal C$, $$\label{rew:obs:two-tests} H(\Psi(g))=0 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad H\bigl((\Psi(g))^*\bigr)=0.$$ Then $U_0\notin\mathop{\mathrm{Thick}}_{\mathcal C}(T_0)$.
  - Explanation: Torsion homology plus the homology-to-dual-homology implication excludes membership in the thick closure.
- **lemma-periodic-splitting** (theorem, [source](#stmt-obs-periodic-splitting)): Let $S_0$ be a domain whose left and right ideals are principal, and let $D_0$ be a positive even integer. Every finitely generated right $S_0$-module is a direct sum of a torsion module and a finite free module and has a finite free resolution of length at most one. Every perfect right dg module over $S_0[t^{\pm1}]$, with $\|t\|=D_0$ and zero differential, splits in its derived category into the $D_0$-periodizations of its shifted homology modules. In particular it splits as a finite sum of free shifts and a perfect module with torsion homology.
  - Explanation: Over a principal-ideal domain modules split into torsion and free parts, and perfect periodic modules split.
- **lemma-tower-normal-form** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rew-obs-tower-normal-form)): For an idempotent-complete triangulated category $\mathcal C$ and an object $T_0$, every $P\in\mathop{\mathrm{Thick}}_{\mathcal C}(T_0)$ is a retract of an object $P_0$ admitting a finite tower of triangles $$F_j\longrightarrow P_j\longrightarrow P_{j+1} \longrightarrow F_j[1], \qquad 0\leq j<s,\qquad P_s=0,$$ where each $F_j$ is a finite sum of arbitrary integer shifts of $T_0$.
  - Explanation: Every thick-closure object is a retract of a finite extension tower of free layers.
- **lemma-microlocal-flat** (construction, [source](#stmt-obs-microlocal-flat)): Let $k$ be a finite field, and let $\mathcal A$ be a complete separated filtered $k$-algebra such that $F^n\mathcal A=\mathcal A$ for $n\leq0$ and $$\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\mathcal A=k[z_1,\ldots,z_d,c]$$ as a graded algebra, with all the displayed generators of positive integer degree. There is a filtered algebra $\mathcal S$ and a map $\mathcal A\to\mathcal S$ with the following properties:* 1. *$\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\mathcal S$ is the homogeneous localization of $\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\mathcal A$ obtained by inverting all homogeneous elements outside $(c)$;* 2. *$\mathcal S$ is separated and complete for its exhaustive filtration, which is allowed to have nonzero terms of negative degree;* 3. *$\mathcal S$ is flat as both a left and a right $\mathcal A$-module.* *For a finitely generated good filtered right module $M$, the corresponding microlocalized module is $M\otimes_{\mathcal A}\mathcal S$ and its associated graded is the same homogeneous localization of $\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}M$. The analogous assertion holds for left modules.
  - Explanation: A complete filtered algebra with polynomial graded ring has a two-sided flat microlocalization.
- **lemma-principal-localization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-obs-principal)): The ring $S$ is a domain, every left or right ideal of $S$ is principal, and $S$ has a two-sided Ore division ring of fractions. The operator $a$ constructed in Proposition <a href="#op:operator" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="op:operator">28</a> has no one-sided inverse in $S$. In particular $S/aS\ne0$, and this right $S$-module is torsion.
  - Explanation: The microlocalized ring is a domain with principal ideals and the cone of the operator has nonzero torsion homology.
- **lemma-joint-kernel-growth** (bound, [source](#stmt-obs-growth)): Suppose $a_1,b_1\in\Delta$ satisfy $$\bigl(\sigma(\bar a_1)\bigr)=(C) \quad\text{in }\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\Omega=k[A,B,C], \qquad \sigma(\bar b_1)\notin(C).$$ Fix an integer $L_0\geq0$. There is a constant $c_0$, depending on the fixed chart, $L_0$, $a_1$, and $b_1$, such that for every $n\geq1$ the image of $$\{f\in x^{-L_0}R:a_1(f)=b_1(f)=0\} \longrightarrow K_0/x^nR$$ has $k$-dimension at most $c_0(1+n)^{1/3}$.
  - Explanation: Joint kernels of two integral operators grow at most like a cube root of the precision.
- **lemma-left-reduction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-obs-left-reduction)): Let $a\in\Delta$ be the operator of Proposition <a href="#op:operator" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="op:operator">28</a>, so that $\bar a=U_3$ and $\sigma(\bar a)=C$.* 1. *Suppose a right ideal $J\subset\Delta$ contains $a\Delta$, is right $x$-saturated, meaning $bx\in J\Rightarrow b\in J$, and satisfies $JS=S$. Then $J$ contains an element $b$ such that $\sigma(\bar b)\notin(C)$.* 2. *Suppose $b_0\in\Delta[x^{-1}]$ does not belong to $Sa$ and annihilates a $k$-subspace $V\subset\ker(a:K_0\to K_0)$. There is $b_1\in\Delta$ annihilating $V$ with $\sigma(\bar b_1)\notin(C)$.
  - Explanation: Saturated ideals and annihilators not accounted for by the operator contain elements with initial symbol outside the ideal of C.
- **lemma-adjoint-laurent** (theorem, [source](#stmt-obs-adjoint)): Every element $b\in\Delta$ has an adjoint $b^\dagger\in\Delta$ for the continuous pairing $$\langle u,f\rangle=\mathop{\mathrm{CT}}_x(uf),\qquad u,f\in K_0.$$ Here $\mathop{\mathrm{CT}}_x$ extracts the coefficient of $x^0$ in a Laurent series. Modulo $x$, taking the adjoint induces group inversion on $\Omega=k[[H]]$. In particular it preserves the conditions that an initial symbol generate $(C)$ or lie outside $(C)$.
  - Explanation: Every integral operator has an adjoint inducing group inversion modulo x and preserving symbol conditions.
- **prop-dual-ghost-detection** (theorem, [source](#stmt-obs-dual-ghost)): For the object $U$ of Proposition <a href="#glue:object" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="glue:object">32</a>, let $g:U\to P$ be a map in $\mathbf D(\mathcal N)$ with $P\in\mathop{\mathrm{Thick}}(T')$. Then $$\label{obs:ghost-implication} H(\widehat g_{\mathscr B})=0 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad H\bigl((\widehat g_{\mathscr B})^*\bigr)=0.$$ Both zero assertions mean that the corresponding induced homology maps vanish.
  - Explanation: A global map from the glued object that vanishes on homology also vanishes on dual homology.
- **thm-nonmembership** (theorem, [source](#stmt-obs-nonmembership)): For the object $U\in\mathbf D(\mathcal N)$ constructed in Proposition <a href="#glue:object" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="glue:object">32</a>, $$U\notin\mathop{\mathrm{Thick}}_{\mathbf D(\mathcal N)}(T').$$
  - Explanation: The glued object is not a finite construction from the chart sheaf.
- **thm-large-prime-algebraicity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-comp-algebraicity)): For $p>8$ there is a triangulated equivalence $$\mathcal F:h\mathrm{Sp}_E\longrightarrow \mathbf D(\mathcal M)$$ whose sheaf homology is naturally identified, under descent and the periodic regrading below, with the $E_*E$-comodule homology functor $E_*(-)$. Its suspension structure gives natural isomorphisms $$\mathcal F(\Sigma^jZ)\cong(\mathcal F Z)[j] \qquad(j\in\mathbb Z).$$
  - Explanation: For primes above eight, E(2)-local spectra are equivalent to periodic sheaf complexes compatibly with homology and suspension.
- **lemma-height-two-comparison-inputs** (bound, [source](#stmt-rew-comp-height-two-inputs)): Let $\mathscr C_E=\operatorname{Comod}_{E_*E}$ be the ordinary abelian category of all graded comodules. Then $$\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathscr C_E}^{s}(M,N)=0 \qquad(M,N\in\mathscr C_E,\ s>6).$$ This includes every internal grading shift. Let $\mathrm{Syn}_E$ be the Grothendieck prestable infinity-category of spherical hypercomplete sheaves of spaces on the site $\mathrm{Sp}_E^{\mathrm{fp}}$ of finite spectra $P$ with $E_*P$ projective over $E_*$, with covers the single maps surjective on $E$-homology. Its objects are connective synthetic spectra, and each satisfies $$X\simeq\lim_{i\geq0}X_{\leq i}\qquad(X\in\mathrm{Syn}_E).$$ The ordinary unbounded derived infinity-category $\mathcal D_{\mathrm{ord}}(\mathscr C_E)$ is left complete as well. For the topological potential stages of the unit shift algebra on this same $\mathrm{Syn}_E$, and the algebraic potential stages of the periodicity algebra on $\mathcal D_{\mathrm{ord}}(\mathscr C_E)_{\geq0}$, the natural functors are equivalences $$\mathcal M_\infty^{\mathrm{top}}\simeq \lim_\ell\mathcal M_\ell^{\mathrm{top}},\qquad \mathcal M_\infty^{\mathrm{alg}}\simeq \lim_\ell\mathcal M_\ell^{\mathrm{alg}}.$$ These are the two convergence inputs $(\spadesuit5)$ of v2 Propositions 4.4 and 4.8.
  - Explanation: Comodule Ext dimension is bounded and the potential-stage towers converge, supplying the comparison inputs.
- **claim-bp-quotient-comodule** (theorem, [source](#stmt-comp-bp-comodule)): For the regular quotient $b$ just constructed, $$\mathrm{BP}_*b\cong\Gamma_P\otimes_{\eta_R,P}B_2.$$ Under Landweber base change to $E$ and descent to $\mathcal M$, this comodule is the pure sheaf $T_{\mathbb Z}$. The corresponding comodule for $b_p^\wedge$ is $T_{\mathbb Z_p}$. Both sheaves are $p$-monic, and their reductions modulo $p$ equal $T_0$.
  - Explanation: The comodule of the regular quotient is the pure chart sheaf, and its completion gives the p-adic version.
- **claim-standard-steenrod-module** (theorem, [source](#stmt-comp-standard-cohomology)): At an odd prime, the quotient $b$ has the standard Steenrod module $$H^*(b;\mathbb F_p)\cong \mathcal A//E(Q_0,Q_1,Q_2),$$ where $Q_i$ are the Milnor primitives and $E(Q_0,Q_1,Q_2)$ is their exterior subalgebra. In particular its completion is a standard form of $\mathrm{BP}\langle2\rangle_p^\wedge$.
  - Explanation: At an odd prime the quotient has the standard Steenrod module.
- **lemma-sparse-representatives** (theorem, [source](#stmt-comp-sparse)): In a twisted periodic derived category of period $D$, suppose a complex has homology only in one residue modulo $D$, with sheaf $W$ in a chosen representative of that residue. It is isomorphic to the corresponding pure object in either of the following cases:* 1. *$W$ has projective dimension at most $a$ in the Ext sense and $D>a+1$;* 2. *$W$ has injective dimension at most $b$ and $D>b+1$.* *The assertion applies to every shift of the complex.
  - Explanation: A periodic complex with homology in one residue and low dimension is the pure object.
- **claim-comparison-generators** (theorem, [source](#stmt-comp-generators)): For every integer $j$,$$\begin{split} \mathcal F(\Sigma^jL_ES^0)&\cong\mathcal O[j],\\ \mathcal F(\Sigma^jL_Eb)&\cong T_{\mathbb Z}[j],\\ \mathcal F(\Sigma^jL_Eb_p^\wedge)&\cong T_{\mathbb Z_p}[j]. \end{split}$$
  - Explanation: The comparison sends shifts of the sphere, quotient and completion to shifts of the pure sheaves.
- **lemma-thick-transport** (theorem, [source](#stmt-comp-thick-transport)): Let $F:\mathcal C\to\mathcal D$ be an equivalence of the underlying categories of triangulated categories, and let $\mathcal D_0$ be a thick subcategory of $\mathcal D$. Then $$\{X\in\mathcal C: F(\Sigma^jX)\in\mathcal D_0\text{ for every }j\in\mathbb Z\}$$ is a thick subcategory of $\mathcal C$.
  - Explanation: An equivalence of underlying categories transports thick subcategories when membership is tested on all shifts.
- **prop-transfer-criterion** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-transfer-criterion)): Let $p>8$, with $D=2(p-1)>12$, and choose the comparison of Theorem <a href="#comp:algebraicity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="comp:algebraicity">44</a>. Suppose $U\in\mathbf D(\mathcal N)$ satisfies $$\pi_iU\text{ is finite for every }i\in\mathbb Z,\qquad \widehat U\in\mathop{\mathrm{Thick}}(\widehat T'),\qquad U\notin\mathop{\mathrm{Thick}}(T'). \label{transfer:conditions}$$ Choose an $E(2)$-local spectrum $Z_{\rm sp}$ whose image under $\mathcal F$ is $$\widetilde U=i_*e_*r^*U\in\mathbf D(\mathcal M).$$ Then $$X_{\rm sp}=\tau_{\geq0}Z_{\rm sp}$$ is a connective $p$-complete fp-spectrum of fp-type at most $2$, and $$L_{E(2)}X_{\rm sp}\simeq Z_{\rm sp},\qquad X_{\rm sp}\notin \mathop{\mathrm{Thick}}_{\mathrm{Sp}_p^\wedge}(b_p^\wedge).$$ Here $b$ is the standard regular quotient of Proposition <a href="#comp:standard-cohomology" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="comp:standard-cohomology">47</a>.
  - Explanation: An algebraic object with finite homotopy, complete finite construction and no global finite construction yields a connective fp-spectrum of fp-type at most two outside the thick closure.
- **lemma-reduction-triangle** (construction, [source](#stmt-transfer-reduction)): There is an exact functor $$Li^*:\mathbf D(\mathcal M)\longrightarrow \mathbf D(\mathcal M_0)$$ which on a termwise $p$-monic complex is ordinary reduction modulo $p$. For $F\in\mathbf D(\mathcal M_0)$ it has a natural comparison triangle $$F[1]\longrightarrow Li^*i_*F\longrightarrow F \xrightarrow{\delta_F}F[2]. \label{transfer:reduction-triangle}$$
  - Explanation: Derived reduction modulo p is exact with a natural comparison triangle.
- **lemma-nilpotent-obstructions** (theorem, [source](#stmt-transfer-nilpotence)): Let $\delta:\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\to[2]$ be a natural transformation on a triangulated category. For an object $A$, consider the property that there is an integer $N\geq1$ such that $$\delta_{A[j]}^N:A[j]\longrightarrow A[j+2N] \quad\text{is zero for every }j\in\mathbb Z,$$ where the power denotes the successive natural transformations on the intervening shifts. No compatibility between $\delta_{A[j]}$ and $\delta_A[j]$ is assumed. This property is thick.* *Suppose in addition that the cones of the maps $\delta_{A[j]}$ are shifts of $B(A[j])$, where $B$ is an exact functor. If $A$ has the property, then $A\in\mathop{\mathrm{Thick}}(B(A))$.
  - Explanation: Uniform nilpotence of a degree-two transformation is thick and recovers thick membership.
- **lemma-central-completion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-transfer-central-completion)): Suppose $U\in\mathbf D(\mathcal N)$ and $\widehat U\in\mathop{\mathrm{Thick}}(\widehat T')$. Set $F=e_*r^*U\in\mathbf D(\mathcal M_0)$. Then $$\widehat F\in\mathop{\mathrm{Thick}}(\widehat T_0).$$ The comparison obstructions of <a href="#transfer:center-triangle" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="transfer:center-triangle">[transfer:center-triangle]</a> and <a href="#transfer:reduction-triangle" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="transfer:reduction-triangle">[transfer:reduction-triangle]</a> are uniformly nilpotent over all shifts of $U$ and $F$, respectively.
  - Explanation: The completed central pullback is a finite construction from the completed generator and the obstructions are uniformly nilpotent.
- **lemma-reduced-exclusion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-transfer-reduced-exclusion)): If $U$ satisfies <a href="#transfer:conditions" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="transfer:conditions">[transfer:conditions]</a>, then $$Li^*(i_*e_*r^*U)\notin\mathop{\mathrm{Thick}}(T_0).$$
  - Explanation: Non-membership survives central pullback, field extension and derived reduction.
- **prop-exact-fp-type-two** (theorem, [source](#stmt-scope-exact-height)): The spectrum $X_{\rm sp}$ has fp-type exactly two.
  - Explanation: The spectrum cannot have fp-type at most one, since the height-one theorem would put it in the thick closure.
- **prop-telescope-comparisons** (theorem, [source](#stmt-scope-telescopes)): There are equivalences $$L_2^fX_{\rm sp}\simeq L_2X_{\rm sp}\simeq Z_{\rm sp}, \qquad L_{T(2)}X_{\rm sp}\simeq L_{K(2)}X_{\rm sp}.$$
  - Explanation: Finite localization agrees with Johnson–Wilson localization and telescopic with Morava localization for the example.
- **cor-local-classification** (theorem, [source](#stmt-scope-local-classification)): The spectrum $Y'=L_{K(2)}X_{\rm sp}$ is locally fp. In the $K(2)$-local category it belongs to $$\mathop{\mathrm{Thick}}(E_2) =\mathop{\mathrm{Thick}}\bigl(L_{K(2)}\mathrm{BP}\langle2\rangle\bigr).$$ Its Morava $E$-cohomology $E_2^*Y'$ is finitely generated over the ring of operations $E_2^*E_2$, and it is reflexive in the $K(2)$-local category.
  - Explanation: The Morava localization is locally fp, lies in the thick closure of Morava E-theory and has finitely generated cohomology.
- **prop-connective-cover-obstruction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-scope-connective-cover)): For height-two Morava $E$-theory, $$\tau_{\geq0}E_2\notin\mathop{\mathrm{Thick}}(b_p^\wedge).$$
  - Explanation: The connective cover of height-two Morava E-theory is not a finite construction from the completed quotient.

        "exact_quote": "The chart law $F_x$ with $v_1=x$, $v_2=1$, and $v_{i>2}=0$ gives compatible presentations $$\\label{geom:quotient-thickenings}\n \\mathcal M_k/(t^d)=[\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spec}}R_d/G_{\\mathrm{up}}],\\qquad\n \\mathcal N/(t^d)=[\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spec}}R_d/G].$$ Under these presentations, $\\mathcal X_k/(t^d)$ is $[\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spec}}R_d/\\Lambda]$. Sheaves are smooth, that is, discrete continuous, semilinear representations over $R_d$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-geom-coinduction",
        "exact_quote": "The sheaves $T'$ and $T_k$ are $t$-monic. Set $T'_d=T'/t^d$ and $T_{k,d}=T_k/t^d$; their ordinary quotients also represent their derived multiplication cofibers. On the chart of Proposition <a href=\"#geom:thickened-charts\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"geom:thickened-charts\">6</a>, $$T'_d=C_{\\mathrm{lc}}(G/\\Lambda,R_d)\n       =C_{\\mathrm{lc}}(H,R_d),$$ with action $$(gF)(s)=g\\bigl(F(g^{-1}s)\\bigr)\n       \\qquad(g\\in G,\\ s\\in G/\\Lambda).$$ The inner value is acted on through the coefficient action on $R_d$. The analogous description of $T_{k,d}$ uses $G_{\\mathrm{up}}/\\Lambda$.",

      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-geom-group-filtration",
        "exact_quote": "The filtration is complete and separated, and $$\\label{geom:group-graded}\n H(i)/H(i+1)=\n \\begin{cases}\n (k,+),&i\\text{ odd},\\\\\n (k/\\mathbb F_p,+),&i\\text{ even},\n \\end{cases}\n \\qquad [H(i),H(j)]\\subset H(i+j).$$ The $p$-th-power map carries each graded digit to the identical digit two indices later. Choose $g_1,g_2$ with first digits an $\\mathbb F_p$-basis of $k$, and choose $g_3=\\gamma$ with only nonzero digit $t_2=\\theta\\notin\\mathbb F_p$. Then $$(a_1,a_2,a_3)\\longmapsto g_1^{a_1}g_2^{a_2}g_3^{a_3}$$ gives unique coordinates $\\mathbb Z_p^3\\to H$. Modulo $\\Gamma_s$, the coordinates are taken modulo $p^s$. In particular $\\Gamma_s$ consists of coordinates divisible by $p^s$, and $H(2)$ consists of coordinates whose first two entries are divisible by $p$. The element $\\gamma$ commutes with $\\Lambda$.",

      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-geom-skew-filtration",
        "exact_quote": "The ring $\\Delta$ has a complete separated decreasing filtration given by the ordered weights $$\\operatorname{wt}(x)=4,\\qquad\n \\operatorname{wt}(U_1)=\\operatorname{wt}(U_2)=1,\\qquad\n \\operatorname{wt}(U_3)=3.$$ Its associated graded is the commutative polynomial ring $$\\label{geom:graded-operations}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{gr}}\\Delta=k[\\sigma x,A,B,C].$$ The element $x$ is normal, $\\Omega=\\Delta/(x)$, and the induced filtration gives $\\mathop{\\mathrm{gr}}\\Omega=k[A,B,C]$. Here $A,B,C$ are the initial symbols of $U_1,U_2,U_3$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-geom-injective-model",
        "exact_quote": "For each flat affine groupoid stack used here, twisted periodic complexes admit the injective model structure with quasi-isomorphisms as weak equivalences and monomorphisms as cofibrations. It is stable, its fibrant objects have injective sheaf terms, and maps into a fibrant object are computed up to compatible chain homotopy. If a sheaf $W$ satisfies $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Ext}}^{>a}(W,-)=0$ for some finite $a$, maps from its pure shifts can be computed by applying $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}(W,-)$ termwise to any quasi-isomorphic replacement whose terms are $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}(W,-)$-acyclic.",
        "source_id": "PM-319.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-geom-injective-model",
          "exact_quote": "For each flat affine groupoid stack used here, twisted periodic complexes admit the injective model structure with quasi-isomorphisms as weak equivalences and monomorphisms as cofibrations.           "source_id": "PM-319.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For each flat affine groupoid stack used here, twisted periodic complexes admit the injective model structure with quasi-isomorphisms as weak equivalences and monomorphisms as cofibrations. If a sheaf $W$ satisfies $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Ext}}^{>a}(W,-)=0$ for some finite $a$, maps from its pure shifts can be computed by applying $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}(W,-)$ termwise to any quasi-isomorphic replacement whose terms are $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}(W,-)$-acyclic.",
      "explanation": "Twisted periodic complexes carry a stable injective model structure computing maps by chain homotopy.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-geom-cohomological-bounds",
        "exact_quote": "Projective dimensions in the Ext sense satisfy $$\\label{geom:dimension-bounds}\n \\operatorname{pd}_{\\mathcal N}(\\mathcal O)\\leq4,\\qquad\n \\operatorname{pd}_{\\mathcal M_k}(\\mathcal O),\n \\operatorname{pd}_{\\mathcal M_0}(\\mathcal O)\\leq5,\\qquad\n \\operatorname{pd}_{\\mathcal M}(\\mathcal O)\\leq6.$$ For sheaves on $\\mathcal M_k$, the right adjoint of $r^*$ has $$\\label{geom:central-derived-invariants}\n r_*M=\\ker(c-1:M\\to M),\\qquad\n R^1r_*M=\\mathop{\\mathrm{coker}}(c-1:M\\to M),\\qquad R^{>1}r_*M=0,$$ where the kernel and cokernel descend to $\\mathcal N$.",
        "source_id": "PM-319.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-geom-cohomological-bounds",
          "exact_quote": "Projective dimensions in the Ext sense satisfy $$\\label{geom:dimension-bounds}\n \\operatorname{pd}_{\\mathcal N}(\\mathcal O)\\leq4,\\qquad\n \\operatorname{pd}_{\\mathcal M_k}(\\mathcal O),\n \\operatorname{pd}_{\\mathcal M_0}(\\mathcal O)\\leq5,\\qquad\n \\operatorname{pd}_{\\mathcal M}(\\mathcal O)\\leq6.$$ For sheaves on $\\mathcal M_k$, the right adjoint of $r^*$ has $$\\label{geom:central-derived-invariants}\n r_*M=\\ker(c-1:M\\to M),\\qquad\n R^1r_*M=\\mathop{\\mathrm{coker}}(c-1:M\\to M),\\qquad R^{>1}r_*M=0,$$ where the kernel and cokernel descend to $\\mathcal N$.",
          "source_id": "PM-319.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Projective dimensions in the Ext sense satisfy $$\\label{geom:dimension-bounds}\n \\operatorname{pd}_{\\mathcal N}(\\mathcal O)\\leq4,\\qquad\n \\operatorname{pd}_{\\mathcal M_k}(\\mathcal O),\n \\operatorname{pd}_{\\mathcal M_0}(\\mathcal O)\\leq5,\\qquad\n \\operatorname{pd}_{\\mathcal M}(\\mathcal O)\\leq6.$$ For sheaves on $\\mathcal M_k$, the right adjoint of $r^*$ has $$\\label{geom:central-derived-invariants}\n r_*M=\\ker(c-1:M\\to M),\\qquad\n R^1r_*M=\\mathop{\\mathrm{coker}}(c-1:M\\to M),\\qquad R^{>1}r_*M=0,$$ where the kernel and cokernel descend to $\\mathcal N$.",

        "anchor": "stmt-geom-relative-pushforward",
        "exact_quote": "Choose an affine law chart $l:S\\to\\mathcal M_k$, set $I=l_*l^*$, and let $J$ be the cokernel of its unit. The two-term totalization $$\\label{geom:Q-definition}\n Q=\\bigl[r_*I\\longrightarrow r_*J\\bigr],\n \\qquad\\text{in homological degrees }0,-1,$$ computes $Rr_*$ on twisted periodic complexes and preserves quasi-isomorphisms. There are natural identifications $$QT_k\\simeq T',\\qquad\n \\pi_*(r^*U)\\cong\\pi_*(Qr^*U)\\quad(U\\in\\mathbf D(\\mathcal N)),$$ and natural triangles $$\\label{geom:central-triangle}\n U\\longrightarrow Qr^*U\\longrightarrow U[-1]\n                     \\longrightarrow U[1].$$",
        "source_id": "PM-319.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-complete-fracture",
        "exact_quote": "The functor $L$ is the flat affine restriction and pushforward for the open $t\\ne 0$, whose derived category it embeds fully faithfully. Completion is exact and idempotent. Its values are right orthogonal, in every shift, to $t$-invertible objects. For every $P$, the fiber of $P\\to\\widehat P$ is $t$-invertible, and there are natural equivalences $$P/t^d\\simeq\\widehat P/t^d \\qquad(d\\geq 1).$$ The following square is a homotopy pullback: $$\\label{complete:fracture-square}\n\\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=large,row sep=large]\n P \\arrow[r] \\arrow[d] & \\widehat P \\arrow[d]\\\\\n LP \\arrow[r] & L\\widehat P.\n\\end{tikzcd}$$ Thus the original object is recovered by matching its open and complete parts over the localized completion. Moreover, $$\\label{complete:localization-sections}\n \\pi_i LP=\\mathop{\\mathrm{colim}}_{s\\geq 0}\\pi_{i+sD}P.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-319.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-complete-fracture",
          "exact_quote": "The functor $L$ is the flat affine restriction and pushforward for the open $t\\ne 0$, whose derived category it embeds fully faithfully. Completion is exact and idempotent. Moreover, $$\\label{complete:localization-sections}\n \\pi_i LP=\\mathop{\\mathrm{colim}}_{s\\geq 0}\\pi_{i+sD}P.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-319.1"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-complete-open-splitting",
        "exact_quote": "The category $\\mathbf D(\\mathcal N[1/t])$ is the category of $D$-periodic complexes of $k$-vector spaces. Every object is the sum, over the $D$ residue classes, of its pure homology objects, and every graded periodic map on homology is realizable.*\n\n*The category $\\mathbf D(\\mathcal M_0[1/t])$ is the category of $D$-periodic complexes of smooth $Z$-representations over $\\mathbb F_p$. Every object also splits as the sum of its pure homology objects. Maps can have an $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Ext}}^1$ component, but a pure object has no self-map to its $2$-shift when $D>3$.",
        "source_id": "PM-319.1"
      },

        }

        "anchor": "stmt-complete-operations",
        "exact_quote": "There is an identification of graded algebras $$\\label{complete:operation-algebra}\n \\mathscr E_{sD}=(x^{-s}\\Delta)^\\Lambda\\quad(s\\in\\mathbb Z),\n \\qquad\n \\mathscr E_n=0\\quad(n\\notin D\\mathbb Z).$$ Products use left-coefficient skew convolution in $\\Delta[x^{-1}]$, with degrees added. Every element of $\\mathscr E_{sD}$ has a unique compatible system of ordinary sheaf-map representatives $T'_d\\to T'_d(s)$, and these representatives realize multiplication strictly. Under <a href=\"#complete:operation-algebra\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"complete:operation-algebra\">[complete:operation-algebra]</a>, the operation $t$ of degree $D$ is the coefficient $1\\in x^{-1}\\Delta$; it is central in $\\mathscr E$.*\n\n\n*Put $$A_{\\mathrm{op}}=(\\Delta[x^{-1}])^\\Lambda.$$ With a formal $t$ recording degree $D$, localization identifies $$\\label{complete:localized-operations}\n \\mathscr E[t^{-1}]=A_{\\mathrm{op}}[t^{\\pm1}].$$",
        "source_id": "PM-319.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-complete-sections",
        "exact_quote": "If $Y=\\mathcal R(\\mathsf P)$ for a perfect right dg $\\mathscr E$-module $\\mathsf P$, then naturally $$\\label{complete:tensor-formulas}\n \\begin{split}\n \\pi_*Y&=H_*\\bigl(\\mathsf P\\otimes^{\\mathbf L}_{\\mathscr E}\n                           \\mathsf L\\bigr),\\\\\n W(Y)&=H_*\\bigl(\\mathsf P\\otimes^{\\mathbf L}_{\\mathscr E}\n                           \\Phi[t^{\\pm1}]\\bigr).\n \\end{split}$$ For a finite-cell model the ordinary cellular tensor computes each derived tensor.         "anchor": "stmt-complete-reductions",
        "exact_quote": "For a finite-cell realization $Y=\\mathop{\\mathrm{holim}}_eY_e$ there are natural equivalences $$Y/t^d\\simeq Y_d\\qquad(d\\geq1).$$ Under these equivalences, the tower projection is the intrinsic reduction map.         "source_id": "PM-319.1"

        "anchor": "stmt-complete-topology",
        "exact_quote": "Every $P\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{Thick}}(T')$ has finite $\\pi_iP$ for every integer $i$. Now let $P\\in\\mathbf D(\\mathcal N)$ have finite $\\pi_iP$ for every integer $i$. The vector space $W_i(\\widehat P)=\\pi_iL\\widehat P$ has a locally compact Hausdorff $k$-vector-space topology obtained as the increasing union of the compact open images of $\\pi_{i+sD}\\widehat P$, for $s\\geq0$. The image of $\\pi_iLP$ is discrete and cocompact in this space.*\n\n*If $Q\\in\\mathbf D(\\mathcal N)$ also has finite $\\pi_jQ$ for every integer $j$, then every map $f:\\widehat P\\to\\widehat Q$ induces continuous maps $\\pi_i\\widehat P\\to\\pi_i\\widehat Q$ and $W_i(\\widehat P)\\to W_i(\\widehat Q)$ for every integer $i$, in the topologies just described. No global map $P\\to Q$ inducing $f$ is required.         "anchor": "stmt-op-matching",
        "exact_quote": "For such a matrix, the numbers $$\\mathsf R(l,j)-\\mathsf R(l,j-1)-\\mathsf R(l+1,j)\n                       +\\mathsf R(l+1,j-1)\n \\label{op:rank-matching}$$ are $0$ or $1$ and define a partial matching of rows with columns. The rank $\\mathsf R(l,j)$ counts the matched pairs in its block. When entries are fixed in order of increasing depth $b=j-l$, a new match at $(l,j)$ is possible exactly when both its row and column are still unmatched and the corner entry avoids one specified scalar.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-op-operator",
        "exact_quote": "Under <a href=\"#op:parameters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"op:parameters\">[op:parameters]</a>, there is $a\\in\\mathscr E_0$ satisfying <a href=\"#op:initial-symbol\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"op:initial-symbol\">[op:initial-symbol]</a> whose triangular matrix on $\\Phi$ has the following properties.*\n\n1.  *For every fixed $J\\in\\mathbb Z$, only finitely many matched pairs $(l,j)$ satisfy $l\\le J<j$.*\n\n2.  *There are constants $c>0$ and $N_0$ such that for every integer $N\\ge N_0$, at least $cN^\\beta$ columns in $[-N,0]$ and at least $cN^\\beta$ rows in $(0,N]$ are forever unmatched.*\n\n*Here the matching is that of Lemma <a href=\"#op:matching\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"op:matching\">27</a>; the constants may depend on $p$.",

        "exact_quote": "An integer $j$ is an unmatched column of $a$ if and only if there is a vector in $\\mathcal K$ with leading term $w^j$. Moreover, for every $J\\in\\mathbb Z$, $$\\dim_k\\frac{a\\Phi\\cap F_J\\Phi}{a(F_J\\Phi)}\n   =\\#\\{(l,j)\\text{ matched}:l\\le J<j\\}<\\infty.\n \\label{glue:crossing-quotient}$$ The image $a\\Phi$ is closed.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-rew-obs-tower-criterion",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal C$ be an idempotent-complete triangulated category, let $T_0,U_0\\in\\mathcal C$, and let $$\\Psi:\\mathcal C\\longrightarrow\\mathop{\\mathrm{Perf}}(\\mathscr B_0)$$ be an exact functor with $\\Psi(T_0)\\simeq\\mathscr B_0$. Assume that $H(\\Psi(U_0))$ is torsion as a right $S_0$-module in every degree and is nonzero in at least one degree. Assume also that, for every $P\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{Thick}}_{\\mathcal C}(T_0)$ and every map $g:U_0\\to P$ in $\\mathcal C$, $$\\label{rew:obs:two-tests}\n H(\\Psi(g))=0\n \\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad\n H\\bigl((\\Psi(g))^*\\bigr)=0.$$ Then $U_0\\notin\\mathop{\\mathrm{Thick}}_{\\mathcal C}(T_0)$.",

      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-obs-periodic-splitting",
        "exact_quote": "Let $S_0$ be a domain whose left and right ideals are principal, and let $D_0$ be a positive even integer. Every finitely generated right $S_0$-module is a direct sum of a torsion module and a finite free module and has a finite free resolution of length at most one. Every perfect right dg module over $S_0[t^{\\pm1}]$, with $|t|=D_0$ and zero differential, splits in its derived category into the $D_0$-periodizations of its shifted homology modules. In particular it splits as a finite sum of free shifts and a perfect module with torsion homology.",
        "source_id": "PM-319.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-obs-periodic-splitting",
          "exact_quote": "Let $S_0$ be a domain whose left and right ideals are principal, and let $D_0$ be a positive even integer. In particular it splits as a finite sum of free shifts and a perfect module with torsion homology.",
          "source_id": "PM-319.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $S_0$ be a domain whose left and right ideals are principal, and let $D_0$ be a positive even integer.         "exact_quote": "For an idempotent-complete triangulated category $\\mathcal C$ and an object $T_0$, every $P\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{Thick}}_{\\mathcal C}(T_0)$ is a retract of an object $P_0$ admitting a finite tower of triangles $$F_j\\longrightarrow P_j\\longrightarrow P_{j+1}\n      \\longrightarrow F_j[1],\n \\qquad 0\\leq j<s,\\qquad P_s=0,$$ where each $F_j$ is a finite sum of arbitrary integer shifts of $T_0$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-obs-microlocal-flat",
        "exact_quote": "Let $k$ be a finite field, and let $\\mathcal A$ be a complete separated filtered $k$-algebra such that $F^n\\mathcal A=\\mathcal A$ for $n\\leq0$ and $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{gr}}\\mathcal A=k[z_1,\\ldots,z_d,c]$$ as a graded algebra, with all the displayed generators of positive integer degree. There is a filtered algebra $\\mathcal S$ and a map $\\mathcal A\\to\\mathcal S$ with the following properties:*\n\n1.  *$\\mathop{\\mathrm{gr}}\\mathcal S$ is the homogeneous localization of $\\mathop{\\mathrm{gr}}\\mathcal A$ obtained by inverting all homogeneous elements outside $(c)$;*\n\n2.  *$\\mathcal S$ is separated and complete for its exhaustive filtration, which is allowed to have nonzero terms of negative degree;*\n\n3.  *$\\mathcal S$ is flat as both a left and a right $\\mathcal A$-module.*\n\n*For a finitely generated good filtered right module $M$, the corresponding microlocalized module is $M\\otimes_{\\mathcal A}\\mathcal S$ and its associated graded is the same homogeneous localization of $\\mathop{\\mathrm{gr}}M$.         "source_id": "PM-319.1"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-obs-adjoint",
        "exact_quote": "Every element $b\\in\\Delta$ has an adjoint $b^\\dagger\\in\\Delta$ for the continuous pairing $$\\langle u,f\\rangle=\\mathop{\\mathrm{CT}}_x(uf),\\qquad u,f\\in K_0.$$ Here $\\mathop{\\mathrm{CT}}_x$ extracts the coefficient of $x^0$ in a Laurent series.         "anchor": "stmt-rew-comp-height-two-inputs",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathscr C_E=\\operatorname{Comod}_{E_*E}$ be the ordinary abelian category of all graded comodules. Then $$\\operatorname{Ext}_{\\mathscr C_E}^{s}(M,N)=0\n \\qquad(M,N\\in\\mathscr C_E,\\ s>6).$$ This includes every internal grading shift. Let $\\mathrm{Syn}_E$ be the Grothendieck prestable infinity-category of spherical hypercomplete sheaves of spaces on the site $\\mathrm{Sp}_E^{\\mathrm{fp}}$ of finite spectra $P$ with $E_*P$ projective over $E_*$, with covers the single maps surjective on $E$-homology. Its objects are connective synthetic spectra, and each satisfies $$X\\simeq\\lim_{i\\geq0}X_{\\leq i}\\qquad(X\\in\\mathrm{Syn}_E).$$ The ordinary unbounded derived infinity-category $\\mathcal D_{\\mathrm{ord}}(\\mathscr C_E)$ is left complete as well. For the topological potential stages of the unit shift algebra on this same $\\mathrm{Syn}_E$, and the algebraic potential stages of the periodicity algebra on $\\mathcal D_{\\mathrm{ord}}(\\mathscr C_E)_{\\geq0}$, the natural functors are equivalences $$\\mathcal M_\\infty^{\\mathrm{top}}\\simeq\n       \\lim_\\ell\\mathcal M_\\ell^{\\mathrm{top}},\\qquad\n \\mathcal M_\\infty^{\\mathrm{alg}}\\simeq\n       \\lim_\\ell\\mathcal M_\\ell^{\\mathrm{alg}}.$$ These are the two convergence inputs $(\\spadesuit5)$ of v2 Propositions 4.4 and 4.8.",
        "source_id": "PM-319.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

For the topological potential stages of the unit shift algebra on this same $\\mathrm{Syn}_E$, and the algebraic potential stages of the periodicity algebra on $\\mathcal D_{\\mathrm{ord}}(\\mathscr C_E)_{\\geq0}$, the natural functors are equivalences $$\\mathcal M_\\infty^{\\mathrm{top}}\\simeq\n       \\lim_\\ell\\mathcal M_\\ell^{\\mathrm{top}},\\qquad\n \\mathcal M_\\infty^{\\mathrm{alg}}\\simeq\n       \\lim_\\ell\\mathcal M_\\ell^{\\mathrm{alg}}.$$ These are the two convergence inputs $(\\spadesuit5)$ of v2 Propositions 4.4 and 4.8.",

        "anchor": "stmt-comp-sparse",
        "exact_quote": "In a twisted periodic derived category of period $D$, suppose a complex has homology only in one residue modulo $D$, with sheaf $W$ in a chosen representative of that residue. It is isomorphic to the corresponding pure object in either of the following cases:*\n\n1.  *$W$ has projective dimension at most $a$ in the Ext sense and $D>a+1$;*\n\n2.  *$W$ has injective dimension at most $b$ and $D>b+1$.*\n\n*The assertion applies to every shift of the complex.",
        "source_id": "PM-319.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-comp-sparse",

          "source_id": "PM-319.1"
        }
      ],

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-transfer-criterion",
        "exact_quote": "Let $p>8$, with $D=2(p-1)>12$, and choose the comparison of Theorem <a href=\"#comp:algebraicity\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"comp:algebraicity\">44</a>. Suppose $U\\in\\mathbf D(\\mathcal N)$ satisfies $$\\pi_iU\\text{ is finite for every }i\\in\\mathbb Z,\\qquad\n \\widehat U\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{Thick}}(\\widehat T'),\\qquad\n U\\notin\\mathop{\\mathrm{Thick}}(T').\n \\label{transfer:conditions}$$ Choose an $E(2)$-local spectrum $Z_{\\rm sp}$ whose image under $\\mathcal F$ is $$\\widetilde U=i_*e_*r^*U\\in\\mathbf D(\\mathcal M).$$ Then $$X_{\\rm sp}=\\tau_{\\geq0}Z_{\\rm sp}$$ is a connective $p$-complete fp-spectrum of fp-type at most $2$, and $$L_{E(2)}X_{\\rm sp}\\simeq Z_{\\rm sp},\\qquad\n X_{\\rm sp}\\notin\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Thick}}_{\\mathrm{Sp}_p^\\wedge}(b_p^\\wedge).$$ Here $b$ is the standard regular quotient of Proposition <a href=\"#comp:standard-cohomology\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"comp:standard-cohomology\">47</a>.",
        "source_id": "PM-319.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-transfer-criterion",
          "exact_quote": "Let $p>8$, with $D=2(p-1)>12$, and choose the comparison of Theorem <a href=\"#comp:algebraicity\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"comp:algebraicity\">44</a>. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **type of a finite spectrum** ([source](#pm-h-3)): A nonzero finite $p$-local spectrum has *type* $j$ if $j$ is the least index for which its $K(j)$-homology is nonzero.
- **fp-spectrum** ([source](#pm-h-3)): A bounded-below $p$-complete spectrum $X$ is *fp* if $H^*(X;\mathbb F_p)$ is finitely presented over the mod-$p$ Steenrod algebra.
- **exact fp-type two** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Exact fp-type two means fp-type at most two but not at most one.
- **ordinary thick closure** ([source](#eq:hw-conjecture)): For a $p$-complete spectrum $B$, let $\mathop{\mathrm{Thick}}(B)$ be its closure in $\mathrm{Sp}_p^\wedge$ under equivalences, finite sums, all integer shifts, cofiber sequences and retracts.
- **locally fp spectrum** ([source](#pm-h-4)): A $K(n)$-local spectrum $Y$ is *locally fp* if $\pi_i(F\wedge Y)$ is finite for every integer $i$ and every finite type-$n$ spectrum $F$.
- **periodicity degree, weights and q** ([source](#geom:section)): Put $$D=2(p-1),\qquad m_i=\frac{p^i-1}{p-1},\qquad q=m_2=p+1.$$
- **t-invertible and complete objects** ([source](#pm-h-16)): We call an object *$t$-invertible* if multiplication $t:P[D]\to P$ is an equivalence, and *complete* if $P\to\widehat P$ is an equivalence.
- **finite-cell dg module** ([source](#pm-h-18)): A *finite-cell right dg $\mathscr E$-module* is a finite iterated extension of shifts of the free right module $\mathscr E$.
- **homology ghost** ([source](#pm-h-33)): A *homology ghost* here is a map inducing zero on module homology in every degree.

      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "t-invertible and complete objects",
      "scope": "Periodic derived categories on characteristic-p stacks."
    },
    {

        }

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **st-charts** [claim](#stmt-geom-law-charts) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geom-law-charts): Landweber flatness, affineness of arrow schemes and coverage on geometric points -> flat affine chart
- **st-torsor** [claim](#stmt-geom-torsor) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geom-torsor): descent along finite quotients and passage to the limit -> torsor over invariants
- **st-rigid** [claim](#stmt-geom-rigidification) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geom-rigidification): quotient of the arrow scheme by vertical composition -> stack N
- **st-thickened** [claim](#stmt-geom-thickened-charts) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geom-thickened-charts): unique lifting of special-fiber labels through nilpotent layers -> presentations by R_d and G
- **st-group-filtration** [claim](#stmt-geom-group-filtration) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geom-group-filtration): digit filtration and compactness -> coordinates on H
- **st-skew-filtration** [claim](#stmt-geom-skew-filtration) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geom-skew-filtration): ordered monomials and weight gain on commutators -> polynomial associated graded ring
- **st-group-cohomology** [claim](#stmt-geom-group-cohomology) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geom-group-cohomology): lift the Koszul resolution of the graded ring -> bounded cohomological dimension
- **st-cohom-bounds** [claim](#stmt-geom-cohomological-bounds) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geom-cohomological-bounds): torsion and localization sequences on each stack -> Ext dimension bounds
- **st-fracture** [claim](#stmt-complete-fracture) / [proof](#proof-stmt-complete-fracture): localization telescope and orthogonality of completion -> fracture square
- **st-operations** [claim](#stmt-complete-operations) / [proof](#proof-stmt-complete-operations): equivariant projective resolution and coinduced measures -> operation algebra
- **st-realization** [claim](#stmt-complete-realization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-complete-realization): strict matrix realization of finite-cell modules -> perfect modules model the thick closure
- **st-displacement** [claim](#stmt-op-displacement) / [proof](#proof-stmt-op-displacement): torsion-point valuations of the deforming formal group -> displacement bound
- **st-divided** [claim](#stmt-op-divided-differences) / [proof](#proof-stmt-op-divided-differences): Lagrange interpolation on balanced orbit points -> integral measures
- **st-operator** [claim](#stmt-op-operator) / [proof](#proof-stmt-op-operator): digit-by-digit pivot choices with coefficient control -> operator with persistent defects
- **st-pivot** [claim](#stmt-glue-pivot-topology) / [proof](#proof-stmt-glue-pivot-topology): compactness argument turning pivots into kernel vectors -> closed image and locally compact kernel and cokernel
- **st-glued-object** [claim](#stmt-glue-object) / [proof](#proof-stmt-glue-object): homotopy pullback along a discrete lattice -> object U
- **st-tower-criterion** [claim](#stmt-rew-obs-tower-criterion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rew-obs-tower-normal-form-2): backward induction through a finite tower -> abstract non-membership criterion
- **st-growth** [claim](#stmt-obs-growth) / [proof](#proof-stmt-obs-growth): Newton interpolation on orbit points and reduction to a finite quotient -> cube-root growth bound
- **st-dual-ghost** [claim](#stmt-obs-dual-ghost) / [proof](#proof-stmt-obs-dual-ghost): compare defect growth with joint kernel growth -> ghosts are dual ghosts
- **st-nonmembership** [claim](#stmt-obs-nonmembership) / [proof](#proof-stmt-obs-nonmembership): apply the finite-tower criterion -> U outside Thick(T')
- **st-algebraicity** [claim](#stmt-comp-algebraicity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rew-comp-height-two-inputs-2): homotopy 2-category comparison with Ext bound and convergence inputs -> triangulated equivalence
- **st-transfer** [claim](#stmt-transfer-criterion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-transfer-reduced-exclusion-2): connective cover of the realized object and reversal of coefficient changes by nilpotence -> fp-spectrum outside the thick closure
- **st-exact-height** [claim](#stmt-scope-exact-height) / [proof](#proof-stmt-scope-exact-height): height-one theorem contradicts the transfer criterion -> exact fp-type two
- **st-telescopes** [claim](#stmt-scope-telescopes) / [proof](#proof-stmt-scope-telescopes): acyclicity of the negative tail and Bousfield class calculation -> localization comparisons
- **st-main** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-scope-telescopes-2): assemble the preceding results at every sufficiently large prime -> main theorem

<!-- PM:BEGIN pm.roadmap.v2 -->

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-rank-double-difference-matching**: For a lower-going triangular matrix, the double difference of the ranks of finite blocks takes only the values 0 or 1, and the positions where it equals 1 form a partial matching of rows with columns.
- **inv-block-rank-counts-pairs**: The rank of each finite block equals the number of matched row-column pairs lying inside that block.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-non-triangular-input**: Feeding a matrix with nonzero entries below the diagonal to the matching procedure and reading its output as a rank matching. -> The block-rank double differences may leave the values 0 and 1, or stop counting matched pairs, so no matching is defined.
- **anti-harness-as-existence-proof**: Treating the passing matching harness as evidence that the counterexample spectrum exists or can be computed. -> The harness only exercises one finite linear-algebra lemma; the spectrum itself comes from an abstract equivalence of categories and infinite completions, which no finite run reproduces.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `algorithmic`. The main theorem is non-constructive, but the rank-matching lemma on finite triangular matrix blocks is a finite procedure: compute block ranks, form double differences, read off the matched pairs. Only that finite step is exercised here.

```python
# INPUTS: stdin tokens: prime p, size n, then n*n integers (row-major) of a triangular matrix over F_p
# PRECONDITIONS: p prime; entries with row index above column index are zero
# OUTPUTS: number of matched pairs, then one line 'l j' per matched pair in lexicographic order
# TIME: O(n^5) from O(n^2) block ranks of cost O(n^3) each
# SPACE: O(n^2)
# ARITHMETIC: exact integers reduced modulo p
# FAILURE: malformed input raises an exception and exits nonzero
import sys

def rank_mod(rows, p):
    m = [r[:] for r in rows]
    rk = 0
    ncols = len(m[0]) if m else 0
    for c in range(ncols):
        piv = None
        for r in range(rk, len(m)):
            if m[r][c] % p:
                piv = r
                break
        if piv is None:
            continue
        m[rk], m[piv] = m[piv], m[rk]
        inv = pow(m[rk][c], -1, p)
        for r in range(rk + 1, len(m)):
            f = m[r][c]*inv%p
            if f:
                for k in range(c, ncols):
                    m[r][k] = (m[r][k] - f*m[rk][k]) % p
        rk += 1
    return rk

def main():
    t = sys.stdin.read().split()
    p = int(t[0])
    n = int(t[1])
    a = [[int(t[2 + i*n + j]) % p for j in range(n)] for i in range(n)]
    def R(l, j):
        if l > j:
            return 0
        return rank_mod([a[i][l:j+1] for i in range(l, j + 1)], p)
    pairs = []
    for l in range(n):
        for j in range(l, n):
            v = R(l, j) - R(l, j-1) - R(l+1, j) + R(l+1, j-1)
            if v == 1:
                pairs.append((l, j))
    print(len(pairs))
    for l, j in pairs:
        print(l, j)

main()
```

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result is a non-constructive existence theorem in chromatic homotopy theory. It specifies no program, data format or interface to implement; the only engineering reading is as a negative constraint on what a library may claim. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Nothing in the statement concerns state evolution, physical dynamics or rule systems. Exact fp-type is a homotopical invariant of a spectrum, with no simulation semantics. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The finite-tower criterion is an abstract statement about triangulated categories and perfect modules over a periodic algebra. It cannot be run on arbitrary objects, so it gives no tool by itself; at most it informs the design of proof-assistant lemmas. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The triangular rank-matching lemma is an exact combinatorial statement, but the operator it is used on is built through infinitely many corrections over a complete skew power series ring. No finite procedure terminating on finite input is given, so no complexity can be claimed. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **lim-prime-range-comparison** (hypothesis): The comparison between E(2)-local spectra and periodic sheaf complexes is only available above the stated prime bound. The counterexample is therefore proved only for primes in that range, and nothing is claimed at the remaining primes. Blocked use: ['Applying the counterexample or the transfer criterion at primes at or below eight', 'Asserting that the Hahn-Wilson conjecture fails at every prime']
- **lim-operator-prime-unquantified** (unknown): The operator construction needs larger primes than the comparison, and the paper gives no explicit numerical threshold, only that the parameter conditions hold at all sufficiently large primes. Blocked use: ['Quoting a concrete smallest prime for which the counterexample is known', 'Using the example at a specific moderately large prime without checking the three parameter inequalities']
- **lim-height-one-theorem** (edge_case): At height one the global generation statement is a theorem, so the height-two construction does not give a counterexample there and the lower heights are classical. Blocked use: ['Reading the result as a failure of global generation at height one', 'Extending the non-membership argument to fp-type at most one']
- **lim-tensor-ideal-scope** (counterexample_scope): The failure of generation concerns the ordinary thick closure of a single generator. It is not a statement about thick tensor ideals of locally dualizable spectra, whose classification has different closure operations. Blocked use: ['Citing the theorem against a thick tensor-ideal classification of dualizable K(n)-local spectra', 'Treating the example as contradicting the local classification at height two']
- **lim-nonconstructive-spectrum-choice** (nonconstructive): The spectrum is chosen as a preimage under an abstract triangulated equivalence, so it is specified only up to the choice of such a preimage and no finite procedure produces it. Blocked use: ['Presenting the spectrum as the output of an algorithm', 'Claiming an explicit cell structure or explicit homotopy groups for the example']
- **lim-comparison-not-monoidal** (formal_scope): The comparison is used only for its homology identification, its distinguished triangles and its retracts. No monoidal or stable infinity-categorical comparison between the topological and algebraic theories is available or used. Blocked use: ['Transporting tensor products or ring structures across the comparison in this argument', 'Quoting the result as a monoidal algebraicity statement']
- **lim-connective-cover-morava-e** (edge_case): For Morava E-theory at height two, the connective cover is not a finite construction from the completed quotient. Taking connective covers of pieces of a local finite construction therefore does not reproduce the height-one argument. Blocked use: ['Deducing global generation by passing to connective covers of K(2)-local finite constructions', 'Reusing the height-one connective-cover step at height two']
- **lim-tower-criterion-torsion** (hypothesis): The finite-tower criterion needs torsion, nonzero homology of the image object, together with the implication from homology vanishing to dual-homology vanishing for maps into the thick closure. Torsion homology alone is not an obstruction. Blocked use: ['Concluding non-membership from torsion homology alone', 'Applying the criterion without verifying the ghost implication for every map in the category']
